<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every bounded Hochschild cocycle of degree at least two on a complex von Neumann algebra, with values in the algebra itself, has a bounded primitive. Together with the established degree-one inner-derivation theorem, this resolves the Kadison–Ringrose cohomology conjecture positively.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $M$ be a complex von Neumann algebra and let $k\geq2$. Every $f\in C_b^k(M,M)$ satisfying $df=0$ has the form $f=dg$ for some $g\in C_b^{k-1}(M,M)$. Consequently $H_b^k(M,M)=0$.
  - Explanation: Establishes actual bounded primitives for all bounded self-coefficient cocycles in the stated higher degrees.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-classical)): Let $M$ be a von Neumann algebra and $k\geq2$.* 1. *If $f\in C_b^k(M,M)$ is a cocycle, there are a separately normal bounded cocycle $f_0$ and a bounded $(k-1)$-cochain $b$ such that $f=f_0+db$.* 2. *If $M$ has no type $\mathrm{II}_1$ central summand, then $H_b^k(M,M)=0$.
  - Explanation: Reduces bounded cocycles to separately normal ones and recalls vanishing on the complementary central summand.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-walk)): Let $M$ be a finite type $\mathrm{II}_1$ von Neumann algebra with separable predual and faithful normal tracial state $\tau$. There exist a symmetric, countably supported probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal U(M)$ and a strictly increasing sequence $n_\ell\to\infty$ with the following properties.* *The positive-mass atoms of $\mu$ contain a countable $2$-norm dense subset of $\mathcal U(M)$, and hence generate $M$ as a von Neumann algebra. For every $r\in\mathbb N$, every nonempty finite set $\mathcal W\subset\mathbb F_r\setminus\{e\}$, and every $\eta>0$, if $G_1^{(\ell)},\ldots,G_r^{(\ell)}$ are independent with law $\mu^{*n_\ell}$, then $$\Pr\left( \max_{w\in\mathcal W} \left\|\tau\bigl(w(G_1^{(\ell)},\ldots,G_r^{(\ell)})\bigr)\right\| \geq\eta \right)\longrightarrow0.$$ Thus these products have joint free Haar moments in probability.
  - Explanation: Constructs a fixed symmetric walk whose independent long products approach free Haar scalar moments along a subsequence.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-popa-special)): Let $P$ be a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor and let $B\subset P^\omega$ be a unital von Neumann subalgebra with separable predual. There is a diffuse abelian von Neumann subalgebra of $P^\omega$ free from $B$. Consequently, for every $d\in\mathbb N$, there are Haar unitaries $u_1,\ldots,u_d\in P^\omega$ such that $B,W^*(u_1),\ldots,W^*(u_d)$ are free.
  - Explanation: Supplies Haar unitaries free from a separable subalgebra in a tracial ultrapower.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distinguished-letters)): Let $B,W^*(u_1),\ldots,W^*(u_d)$ be free subalgebras of a tracial von Neumann algebra, with each $u_j$ Haar. Let $y_1,\ldots,y_r$ be path expressions evaluated at these unitaries, with coefficients in $\mathcal U(B)$. Suppose that for each $i$ there is a label $h_i$ occurring exactly once in the entire list of path expressions, and that its occurrence belongs to $y_i$. Then $$\tau\bigl(w(y_1,\ldots,y_r)\bigr)=0 \qquad(w\in\mathbb F_r\setminus\{e\}).$$
  - Explanation: A unique distinguished label in each path forces nonidentity word traces to vanish.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-path-tests)): Fix a finite set $F\subset\mathcal U(M)$, an integer $d\geq1$, and $\varepsilon>0$. Consider a finite collection of tests. Each test consists of a finite list of path expressions $y_1,\ldots,y_r$ in the alphabet $F\cup\{U_j^{\pm1}:1\leq j\leq d\}$ and a nonidentity word $w\in\mathbb F_r$. Assume that each path in each test has a distinguished label occurring exactly once in that test’s entire list, as in Lemma <a href="#lem:distinguished-letters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:distinguished-letters">5</a>. The distinguished labels may differ between tests. There are $u_1,\ldots,u_d\in\mathcal U(M)$ for which all the tests satisfy $$\left\|\tau\bigl(w(y_1(u),\ldots,y_r(u))\bigr)\right\| <\varepsilon.$$
  - Explanation: Realizes a finite family of trace tests within the prescribed tolerance in the original algebra.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ultrapower)): Let $(N,\rho)$ be as in Definition <a href="#def:ball-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:ball-continuity">8</a>, and let $R:N\to N$ be bounded and linear. Condition <a href="#eq:ball-sequential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ball-sequential">[eq:ball-sequential]</a> is equivalent to the following uniform assertion: for every $r<\infty$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta>0$ such that $$\label{eq:ball-modulus} \norm{x}\le r,\quad \norm{x}_2<\delta \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \norm{R(x)}_2<\varepsilon.$$ If this condition holds and $\omega$ is a free ultrafilter on $\mathbb N$, then $$\label{eq:ultrapower-map} R^\omega:N^\omega\longrightarrow N^\omega, \qquad R^\omega([(x_j)]_\omega)=[(R(x_j))]_\omega,$$ is a well-defined bounded linear map with $\norm{R^\omega}\le\norm{R}$. It is bounded-ball $2$-continuous for the ultrapower trace. In particular, for every free ultrafilter $\nu$, the map $(R^\omega)^\nu$ on $(N^\omega)^\nu$ has the same properties.
  - Explanation: Transfers bounded-ball continuity to tracial ultrapowers with no increase in the operator norm.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-liouville)): Let $(M,\tau)$ be a type $\mathrm{II}_1$ von Neumann algebra with separable predual and a faithful normal tracial state, and let $\mu$ be the measure supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:walk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:walk">3</a>. Suppose that $T:M\to M$ is bounded, linear, and bounded-ball $2$-continuous, and that $$\label{eq:harmonic-map} T(x)=\mathbb E_{U\sim\mu}\bigl[T(xU)U^*\bigr] \qquad(x\in M).$$ Then $$\label{eq:liouville-conclusion} T(x)=T(1)x\qquad(x\in M).$$
  - Explanation: Identifies harmonic bounded-ball continuous maps as left multiplication by their value at the identity.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-rigidity)): Let $(N,\tau)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with a faithful normal tracial state. Let $R:N\to N$ be bounded and complex linear, and suppose that $$\label{eq:rigidity-continuity} \norm{R(x_n)}_2\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{whenever}\quad \sup_n\norm{x_n}<\infty, \qquad \norm{x_n}_2\longrightarrow0.$$ Let $(g_j)_{j\geq1}$ be a free Haar family in $N$, and let $a,b\in N$. Suppose that for every nonempty signed word $w=g_{i_1}^{\varepsilon_1}\cdots g_{i_q}^{\varepsilon_q}$ with $\varepsilon_j\in\{1,-1\}$ and pairwise distinct indices $i_1,\ldots,i_q$, $$\label{eq:rigidity-first-letter} R(w)= \begin{cases} aw,&\varepsilon_1=1,\\ bw,&\varepsilon_1=-1. \end{cases}$$ Then $a=b$.
  - Explanation: Forces the signed first-letter multipliers to agree under finite tracial and continuity hypotheses.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-length)): Let $\mathbb F_r$ be the free group on $r$ generators and let $\lambda$ be its left regular representation. For a finitely supported family $(c_w)_{\|w\|=q}$ on the reduced words of length $q\geq0$, $$\label{eq:rigidity-length} \left\\|\sum_{\|w\|=q}c_w\lambda(w)\right\\| \leq(q+1)\left(\sum_{\|w\|=q}\|c_w\|^2\right)^{1/2}.$$ If $g_1,\ldots,g_r$ have free Haar joint moments in a faithfully tracial von Neumann algebra, every scalar-coefficient word polynomial in them has the same norm as the corresponding polynomial in the regular generators. Consequently, <a href="#eq:rigidity-length" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rigidity-length">[eq:rigidity-length]</a> also holds with $\lambda(w)$ replaced by $w(g)$, and its right-hand side is $(q+1)\norm{\sum_{\|w\|=q}c_w w(g)}_2$.
  - Explanation: Controls homogeneous free-word sums in operator norm and transfers the estimate through faithful traces.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-blocks)): For every $k\geq1$ and $d_1,\ldots,d_k\geq0$, let $$P(z)=B_{d_1}(z)B_{d_2}(z)\cdots B_{d_k}(z).$$ Then $$\label{eq:rigidity-block-identities} S(P(s))=aP(s),\qquad S(P(s)^*)=bP(s)^*.$$
  - Explanation: Propagates first-letter identities to ordered block products and their adjoints.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-polar)): The polar part $V$ of $s$ is a unitary in $\widehat N$, and $$\label{eq:rigidity-power-identities} S(V^k)=aV^k,\qquad S(V^{-k})=bV^{-k} \qquad(k\geq1).$$
  - Explanation: Obtains a unitary polar part and transfers the multiplier identities to all positive and negative powers.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-unitary)): Let $(\mathcal A,\rho)$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state, let $V\in\mathcal A$ be unitary, and let $S:\mathcal A\to\mathcal A$ be bounded and complex linear. If $a,b\in\mathcal A$ satisfy $$S(V^k)=aV^k,\qquad S(V^{-k})=bV^{-k}\qquad(k\geq1),$$ then $a=b$.
  - Explanation: Finite faithful traciality forces the power multipliers to agree, without a continuity assumption.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-two-continuity)): Let $M$ be a finite von Neumann algebra with faithful normal tracial state $\tau$, and let $B:M\to M$ be bounded and normal. For every $R<\infty$ and $\varepsilon>0$ there is $\delta>0$ such that $$\norm{x}\le R,\quad \norm{x}_2<\delta \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \norm{B(x)}_2<\varepsilon.$$ Consequently $B$ is $2$-norm continuous on every operator norm ball.
  - Explanation: Normal bounded linear maps on finite tracial algebras have the required bounded-ball continuity.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-separable-primitive)): Let $M$ be of type $\mathrm{II}_1$ with separable predual and faithful normal tracial state $\tau$. If $k\ge2$ and $f\in C_b^k(M,M)$ is a separately normal cocycle, then there is $g\in C_b^{k-1}(M,M)$ such that $$\label{eq:separable-primitive-bound} dg=f,\qquad \norm{g}\le\norm{f}.$$
  - Explanation: Produces a primitive bounded by the cocycle norm for separately normal cocycles in the separable tracial setting.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-tracial-primitive)): Let $M$ be any type $\mathrm{II}_1$ von Neumann algebra admitting a faithful normal tracial state. Every separately normal cocycle $f\in C_b^k(M,M)$, $k\ge2$, has a bounded primitive $g$ with $\norm{g}\le\norm{f}$.
  - Explanation: Preserves the primitive bound after removing the separability assumption.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-central-homotopy)): For $n\ge2$ and $\psi\in C_b^n(M,zM)$, set $$\label{eq:central-homotopy-formula} \begin{split} (J_z\psi)(a_1,\ldots,a_{n-1}) =\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(-1)^i\psi(&za_1,\ldots,za_{i-1}, q,qa_i,\\[-2pt] &a_{i+1},\ldots,a_{n-1}). \end{split}$$ Set $J_z=0$ in degrees zero and one. Then, in every degree, $$\label{eq:central-homotopy-identity} dJ_z+J_zd=\operatorname{id}-\operatorname{cut}_z.$$ For $n\ge1$ the degree-$n$ operator satisfies $$\label{eq:central-homotopy-bound} \norm{J_z\psi}\le(n-1)\norm{\psi}.$$
  - Explanation: An explicit central homotopy corrects mixed inputs and has a bound independent of the central projection.

        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a von Neumann algebra and $k\\geq2$.*\n\n1.  *If $f\\in C_b^k(M,M)$ is a cocycle, there are a separately normal bounded cocycle $f_0$ and a bounded $(k-1)$-cochain $b$ such that $f=f_0+db$.*\n\n2.  *If $M$ has no type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ central summand, then $H_b^k(M,M)=0$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-walk",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a finite type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ von Neumann algebra with separable predual and faithful normal tracial state $\\tau$. There exist a symmetric, countably supported probability measure $\\mu$ on $\\mathcal U(M)$ and a strictly increasing sequence $n_\\ell\\to\\infty$ with the following properties.*\n\n*The positive-mass atoms of $\\mu$ contain a countable $2$-norm dense subset of $\\mathcal U(M)$, and hence generate $M$ as a von Neumann algebra. For every $r\\in\\mathbb N$, every nonempty finite set $\\mathcal W\\subset\\mathbb F_r\\setminus\\{e\\}$, and every $\\eta>0$, if $G_1^{(\\ell)},\\ldots,G_r^{(\\ell)}$ are independent with law $\\mu^{*n_\\ell}$, then $$\\Pr\\left(\n   \\max_{w\\in\\mathcal W}\n   \\left|\\tau\\bigl(w(G_1^{(\\ell)},\\ldots,G_r^{(\\ell)})\\bigr)\\right|\n   \\geq\\eta\n \\right)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Thus these products have joint free Haar moments in probability.",
        "source_id": "PM-295.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-295.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-popa-special",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a $\\mathrm{II}_1$ factor and let $B\\subset P^\\omega$ be a unital von Neumann subalgebra with separable predual. There is a diffuse abelian von Neumann subalgebra of $P^\\omega$ free from $B$. Consequently, for every $d\\in\\mathbb N$, there are Haar unitaries $u_1,\\ldots,u_d\\in P^\\omega$ such that $B,W^*(u_1),\\ldots,W^*(u_d)$ are free.",
        "source_id": "PM-295.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-distinguished-letters",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B,W^*(u_1),\\ldots,W^*(u_d)$ be free subalgebras of a tracial von Neumann algebra, with each $u_j$ Haar. Let $y_1,\\ldots,y_r$ be path expressions evaluated at these unitaries, with coefficients in $\\mathcal U(B)$. Suppose that for each $i$ there is a label $h_i$ occurring exactly once in the entire list of path expressions, and that its occurrence belongs to $y_i$. Then $$\\tau\\bigl(w(y_1,\\ldots,y_r)\\bigr)=0\n \\qquad(w\\in\\mathbb F_r\\setminus\\{e\\}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-295.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-path-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite set $F\\subset\\mathcal U(M)$, an integer $d\\geq1$, and $\\varepsilon>0$. Consider a finite collection of tests. Each test consists of a finite list of path expressions $y_1,\\ldots,y_r$ in the alphabet $F\\cup\\{U_j^{\\pm1}:1\\leq j\\leq d\\}$ and a nonidentity word $w\\in\\mathbb F_r$. Assume that each path in each test has a distinguished label occurring exactly once in that test’s entire list, as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:distinguished-letters\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:distinguished-letters\">5</a>. The distinguished labels may differ between tests. There are $u_1,\\ldots,u_d\\in\\mathcal U(M)$ for which all the tests satisfy $$\\left|\\tau\\bigl(w(y_1(u),\\ldots,y_r(u))\\bigr)\\right|\n <\\varepsilon.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ultrapower",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(N,\\rho)$ be as in Definition <a href=\"#def:ball-continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:ball-continuity\">8</a>, and let $R:N\\to N$ be bounded and linear. Condition <a href=\"#eq:ball-sequential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ball-sequential\">[eq:ball-sequential]</a> is equivalent to the following uniform assertion: for every $r<\\infty$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ there is $\\delta>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:ball-modulus}\n \\norm{x}\\le r,\\quad \\norm{x}_2<\\delta\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad \\norm{R(x)}_2<\\varepsilon.$$ If this condition holds and $\\omega$ is a free ultrafilter on $\\mathbb N$, then $$\\label{eq:ultrapower-map}\n R^\\omega:N^\\omega\\longrightarrow N^\\omega,\n \\qquad R^\\omega([(x_j)]_\\omega)=[(R(x_j))]_\\omega,$$ is a well-defined bounded linear map with $\\norm{R^\\omega}\\le\\norm{R}$. It is bounded-ball $2$-continuous for the ultrapower trace. In particular, for every free ultrafilter $\\nu$, the map $(R^\\omega)^\\nu$ on $(N^\\omega)^\\nu$ has the same properties.",
        "source_id": "PM-295.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ultrapower",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(N,\\rho)$ be as in Definition <a href=\"#def:ball-continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:ball-continuity\">8</a>, and let $R:N\\to N$ be bounded and linear. Condition <a href=\"#eq:ball-sequential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ball-sequential\">[eq:ball-sequential]</a> is equivalent to the following uniform assertion: for every $r<\\infty$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ there is $\\delta>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:ball-modulus}\n \\norm{x}\\le r,\\quad \\norm{x}_2<\\delta\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad \\norm{R(x)}_2<\\varepsilon.$$ If this condition holds and $\\omega$ is a free ultrafilter on $\\mathbb N$, then $$\\label{eq:ultrapower-map}\n R^\\omega:N^\\omega\\longrightarrow N^\\omega,\n \\qquad R^\\omega([(x_j)]_\\omega)=[(R(x_j))]_\\omega,$$ is a well-defined bounded linear map with $\\norm{R^\\omega}\\le\\norm{R}$. It is bounded-ball $2$-continuous for the ultrapower trace. In particular, for every free ultrafilter $\\nu$, the map $(R^\\omega)^\\nu$ on $(N^\\omega)^\\nu$ has the same properties.",
          "source_id": "PM-295.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $(N,\\rho)$ be as in Definition <a href=\"#def:ball-continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:ball-continuity\">8</a>, and let $R:N\\to N$ be bounded and linear.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-liouville",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M,\\tau)$ be a type $\\mathrm{II}_1$ von Neumann algebra with separable predual and a faithful normal tracial state, and let $\\mu$ be the measure supplied by Theorem <a href=\"#thm:walk\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:walk\">3</a>. Suppose that $T:M\\to M$ is bounded, linear, and bounded-ball $2$-continuous, and that $$\\label{eq:harmonic-map}\n T(x)=\\mathbb E_{U\\sim\\mu}\\bigl[T(xU)U^*\\bigr]\n \\qquad(x\\in M).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:liouville-conclusion}\n T(x)=T(1)x\\qquad(x\\in M).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-295.1"
      },

For a finitely supported family $(c_w)_{|w|=q}$ on the reduced words of length $q\\geq0$, $$\\label{eq:rigidity-length}\n \\left\\|\\sum_{|w|=q}c_w\\lambda(w)\\right\\|\n \\leq(q+1)\\left(\\sum_{|w|=q}|c_w|^2\\right)^{1/2}.$$ If $g_1,\\ldots,g_r$ have free Haar joint moments in a faithfully tracial von Neumann algebra, every scalar-coefficient word polynomial in them has the same norm as the corresponding polynomial in the regular generators. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Bounded-ball tracial continuity** ([source](#stmt-def-ball-continuity)): A linear map $R:N\to N$ is *bounded-ball $2$-continuous* if $$\label{eq:ball-sequential} \sup_j\norm{x_j}<\infty,\qquad \norm{x_j}_2\longrightarrow0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \norm{R(x_j)}_2\longrightarrow0.$$
- **Haar unitary** ([source](#sec:walk)): A unitary $u$ is *Haar* if $\tau(u^k)=0$ for every $k\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$.
- **Free subalgebras** ([source](#sec:walk)): Recall that unital subalgebras of a tracial von Neumann algebra are *free* if every alternating product of centered elements from successively different subalgebras has trace zero.
- **Path expression** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A *path expression* is a finite ordered product of fixed unitary coefficients and formal letters $U_j^{\pm1}$.
- **Separately normal map** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A map $f:M^k\to M$ is *separately normal* if it is ultraweakly continuous in each input with the others fixed.
- **Convolution powers** ([source](#sec:walk)): For a probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathcal U(M)$, let $\mu^{*n}$ be the law of the ordered product $U_1\cdots U_n$ of independent increments of law $\mu$.
- **Symmetric unitary measure** ([source](#sec:walk)): Symmetry means that $U^{-1}$ has the same law as $U$.
- **Type of the finite algebra** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A finite algebra is called type $\mathrm{II}_1$ here when it has no nonzero type I central summand; its center need not be trivial.

| Q13 | Primitive cochain norm | ≤ | $\norm{f}$ | $k\ge2$. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-path-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-path-tests): Measurable finite selection -> Finite unitary configurations
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-walk) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-path-tests-2): Successive rare levels and union bounds -> A fixed walk with asymptotic scalar moments
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-ultrapower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ultrapower): Preservation of the tracial null ideal -> Continuous lifted maps
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-blocks): Discard repeated surviving indices -> Ordered block multiplier identities
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-polar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-polar): Catalan moments and regularized polar approximation -> Power identities for a unitary
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-unitary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-unitary): Bounded sine sums and spectral compression -> Equality of the multipliers

- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-central-homotopy-2): Central partition and bounded direct-product assembly -> Actual bounded primitives on arbitrary complex von Neumann algebras

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **bounded-primitive**: Every bounded self-coefficient cocycle in degree $k\geq2$ has an actual bounded primitive; cohomology uses the actual image of the differential.
- **harmonic-module**: A harmonic bounded linear map with bounded-ball $2$-continuity is left multiplication by its value at the identity.
- **central-homotopy**: The explicit central homotopy satisfies a chain homotopy relation between the identity and the cut operator and corrects mixed central-summand inputs.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **norm-from-moments**: Treat the sampled products as convergent in operator norm. -> Scalar moment convergence does not supply polynomial operator-norm convergence.
- **infinite-trace-rigidity**: Apply the power-multiplier rigidity lemma on an infinite operator algebra. -> The shift compression counterexample admits distinct positive and negative power multipliers.
- **primitive-algorithm**: Treat the bounded primitive theorem as a terminating primitive solver. -> The averaging argument selects a subnet by compactness; a finite terminating selection procedure is not supplied.
- **finite-certificate-overreach**: Use finite central-homotopy tests to certify general cohomology vanishing. -> Finite cochain identities do not certify the random walk, ultrapower rigidity or compactness reductions.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite specialization of the explicit central homotopy; no primitive construction for arbitrary von Neumann algebras is certified.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with z_mask in 0..3 and max_degree in 0..3 on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: coordinatewise algebra Q^2; coefficients lie in the central summand zQ^2.
# OUTPUTS: exact integer matrices of J in degrees 1 through max_degree+1 as JSON.
# TIME: O(N^2 * 4^N), N=max_degree+1, for direct dense matrix assembly.
# SPACE: O(4^N) exact integer entries.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integers, embedded in the rational cochain space.
# FAILURE: invalid input raises ValueError; no approximation or unsupported degree is admitted.
import sys, json, itertools
from flint import fmpz
request = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
z = request['z_mask']; top = request['max_degree']
if type(z) is not int or type(top) is not int or z not in range(4) or top not in range(4):
    raise ValueError('outside finite contract')
outputs = [c for c in range(2) if z & (1 << c)]
result = {}
for n in range(1, top + 2):
    domain = list(itertools.product(range(2), repeat=n))
    target = list(itertools.product(range(2), repeat=n-1))
    columns = {(t,c):j for j,(t,c) in enumerate(itertools.product(domain, outputs))}
    rows = []
    for args, c in itertools.product(target, outputs):
        row = [fmpz(0) for _ in columns]
        for i in range(1, n):
            if not all(z & (1 << a) for a in args[:i-1]):
                continue
            if z & (1 << args[i-1]):
                continue
            for h in range(2):
                if z & (1 << h):
                    continue
                expanded = args[:i-1] + (h, args[i-1]) + args[i:]
                row[columns[(expanded,c)]] += fmpz((-1)**i)
        rows.append([int(v) for v in row])
    result[str(n)] = rows
print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))
```

js.load(open(system.argv[2]))\nmask, maximum = parameters['z_mask'], parameters['max_degree']\nif system.argv[3] != 'exact central homotopy':\n    raise AssertionError('negative expectation')\ncomponents = tuple(t for t in (0,1) if (mask >> t) & 1)\ndef basis(degree):\n    return [(word,coord) for word in cartesian((0,1), repeat=degree) for coord in components]\ndef differential(degree):\n    before, after = basis(degree), basis(degree+1)\n    index = {entry: position for position,entry in enumerate(before)}\n    mat = zeros(len(after), len(before))\n    for row,(word,coord) in enumerate(after):\n        if word[0] == coord:\n            mat[row,index[(word[1:],coord)]] += 1\n        for j in range(degree):\n            if word[j] == word[j+1]:\n                merged = word[:j] + word[j+1:]\n                mat[row,index[(merged,coord)]] += (-1)**(j+1)\n        if word[-1] == coord:\n            mat[row,index[(word[:-1],coord)]] += (-1)**(degree+1)\n    return mat\noperators = {}\nassert set(actual) == {str(degree) for degree in range(1,maximum+2)}\nfor degree in range(1,maximum+2):\n    small, large = basis(degree-1), basis(degree)\n    expected = zeros(len(small),len(large))\n    positions = {entry:i for i,entry in enumerate(large)}\n    for row,(word,coord) in enumerate(small):\n        missing = next((place for place,letter in enumerate(word) if not ((mask >> letter)&1)), None)\n        if missing is not None:\n            for letter in (0,1):\n                if not ((mask >> letter)&1):\n                    inserted = word[:missing] + (letter,) + word[missing:]\n                    expected[row,positions[(inserted,coord)]] = (-1)**(missing+1)\n    encoded = actual[str(degree)]\n    assert isinstance(encoded,list) and len(encoded) == len(small)\n    assert all(isinstance(row,list) and len(row)==len(large) for row in encoded)\n    assert all(type(value) is int for row in encoded for value in row)\n    candidate len(basis(degree))\n    cut = zeros(size,size)\n    for position,(word,coord) in enumerate(basis(degree)):\n        if all((mask >> letter)&1 for letter in word):\n            cut[position,position] = 1\n    composed = operators[degree+1]*differential(degree)\n    if degree:\n        composed += differential(degree-1)*operators[degree]\n    assert composed == eye(size)-cut, 'homotopy identity mismatch'\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact dense matrices of the explicitly defined central homotopy.",

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with z_mask in 0..3 and max_degree in 0..3 on stdin.\n# PRECONDITIONS: coordinatewise algebra Q^2; coefficients lie in the central summand zQ^2.\n# OUTPUTS: exact integer matrices of J in degrees 1 through max_degree+1 as JSON.\n# TIME: O(N^2 * 4^N), N=max_degree+1, for direct dense matrix assembly.\n# SPACE: O(4^N) exact integer entries.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpz exact integers, embedded in the rational cochain space.\n# FAILURE: invalid input raises ValueError; no approximation or unsupported degree is admitted.\nimport sys, json, itertools\nfrom flint import fmpz\nrequest = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nz = request['z_mask']; top = request['max_degree']\nif type(z) is not int or type(top) is not int or z not in range(4) or top not in range(4):\n    raise ValueError('outside finite contract')\noutputs = [c for c in range(2) if z & (1 << c)]\nresult = {}\nfor n in range(1, top + 2):\n    domain = list(itertools.product(range(2), repeat=n))\n    target = list(itertools.product(range(2), repeat=n-1))\n    columns = {(t,c):j for j,(t,c) in enumerate(itertools.product(domain, outputs))}\n    rows = []\n    for args, c in itertools.product(target, outputs):\n        row = [fmpz(0) for _ in columns]\n        for i in range(1, n):\n            if not all(z & (1 << a) for a in args[:i-1]):\n                continue\n            if z & (1 << args[i-1]):\n                continue\n            for h in range(2):\n                if z & (1 << h):\n                    continue\n                expanded = args[:i-1] + (h, args[i-1]) + args[i:]\n                row[columns[(expanded,c)]] += fmpz((-1)**i)\n        rows.append([int(v) for v in row])\n    result[str(n)] = rows\nprint(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The higher-degree vanishing result concerns complex von Neumann algebras with coefficients in the algebra itself and bounded cocycles. Blocked use: ['Transfer to arbitrary Banach algebras or unrelated coefficient modules.', 'Treat an unbounded cochain as a bounded cocycle.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The main theorem assumes $k\geq2$; the degree-one conclusion uses the established inner-derivation theorem separately. Blocked use: ['Attribute the degree-one derivation theorem to the higher-degree proof.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): Cohomology uses the actual image of the differential, without taking its closure. Blocked use: ['Replace existence of an exact bounded primitive by approximate solvability.']
- **L4** (approximation): Random-walk convergence concerns scalar moments in probability along a subsequence; it supplies no operator-norm convergence claim. Blocked use: ['Infer norm convergence of sampled word polynomials or a numerical norm certificate.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The Liouville theorem requires the supplied measure together with boundedness, linearity, and bounded-ball $2$-continuity. Blocked use: ['Apply the conclusion to an arbitrary walk or drop the continuity hypothesis.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The first-letter rigidity identities are imposed on words with pairwise distinct unsigned indices. Blocked use: ['Reuse a path block and invoke the independent-block estimate.', 'Apply the first-letter hypothesis directly to repeated powers of one generator.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): Freeness of the extracted unitaries from the endpoint coefficients is neither asserted nor required. Blocked use: ['Assume the coefficients are free from the generated family or commute with it.']
- **L8** (counterexample_scope): The power-identity rigidity lemma cannot be transferred to an infinite algebra: the paper gives unequal multipliers on $B(H)$. Blocked use: ['Remove the finite faithful tracial setting from the power-identity lemma.']
- **L9** (nonconstructive): Averaging and pointwise ultraweak compactness establish primitive existence; a terminating general extraction procedure and computational complexity bound are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Advertise an executable general primitive solver or an effective convergence rate.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The estimate $\norm{g}\le\norm{f}$ is stated for separately normal cocycles on type $\mathrm{II}_1$ algebras admitting a faithful normal tracial state. Blocked use: ['Export this estimate unchanged to the arbitrary bounded-cocycle conclusion.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): The averaged limiting cochain need not remain separately normal; the proof instead preserves a continuity modulus. Blocked use: ['Require or assert separate normality of the limit without additional evidence.', 'Assume the averaging operator commutes with the differential.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): The explicit central homotopy corrects mixed inputs in a central summand. Finite checks of this identity do not establish the general primitive theorem. Blocked use: ['Treat a finite central-homotopy implementation as a constructive general primitive algorithm.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 295 (`lean/docs/295.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Vanishing of higher bounded Hochschild cohomology](../../preprints/Vanishing-of-higher-bounded-Hochschild-cohomology-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves vanishing of bounded Hochschild cohomology in every degree at least two for a complex von Neumann algebra with coefficients in itself. Every bounded multilinear cocycle of such a degree is the Hochschild differential of a bounded multilinear cochain one degree lower. No separability or type restriction is imposed. The degree-one inner-derivation theorem is outside this selected statement.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Cochain` | abbrev |
| `mergeInputs` | def |
| `differentialValue` | def |
| `main_result` | theorem |

Comparator source `KadisonRingrose.lean` (1329 bytes, sha256 `cf483338b48e3a6cf964d8e37926c171e9c483fa7d11285e6f2f15d7d6fac8fe`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Every ordinary bounded higher Hochschild cocycle of a complex von Neumann
algebra is the coboundary of a bounded cochain with values in the same algebra. -/

noncomputable section

namespace BoundedHochschild

abbrev Cochain (M : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup M] [NormedSpace ℂ M] (n : ℕ) :=
  ContinuousMultilinearMap ℂ (fun _ : Fin n => M) M

def mergeInputs {M : Type*} [Mul M] {n : ℕ}
    (v : Fin (n + 1) → M) (j : Fin n) : Fin n → M :=
  fun i => if i < j then v i.castSucc
    else if i = j then v i.castSucc * v i.succ else v i.succ

noncomputable def differentialValue {M : Type*} [NormedRing M] [NormedAlgebra ℂ M]
    {n : ℕ} (f : Cochain M n) (v : Fin (n + 1) → M) : M :=
  v 0 * f (fun i => v i.succ) +
    ∑ j : Fin n, ((-1 : ℂ) ^ (j.val + 1)) • f (mergeInputs v j) +
    ((-1 : ℂ) ^ (n + 1)) • (f (fun i => v i.castSucc) * v (Fin.last n))

namespace KadisonRingrose

universe u

theorem main_result
    {M : Type u} [CStarAlgebra M] [PartialOrder M] [StarOrderedRing M] [WStarAlgebra M]
    (n : ℕ) (f : Cochain M (n + 2))
    (hf : ∀ x : Fin (n + 3) → M, differentialValue f x = 0) :
    ∃ g : Cochain M (n + 1), ∀ x : Fin (n + 2) → M,
      differentialValue g x = f x := by
  sorry

end KadisonRingrose
end BoundedHochschild

end

end OAI
```

The degree-one inner-derivation theorem is outside this selected statement."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every bounded Hochschild cocycle of degree at least two on a complex von Neumann algebra, with values in the algebra itself, has a bounded primitive. Together with the established degree-one inner-derivation theorem, this resolves the Kadison–Ringrose cohomology conjecture positively.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
